f sample operational CBR weather forecast.
   o. Air Weather Service. The U. S. Air Force Air
Weather Service (AWS) forecasts meteorological
conditions for use by ground and air forces in a
theater of operations. Under current weather fore-
casting capabilities, weather conditions existing at
targets in enemy-held terrain at the time of attack
are predicted from observations made some time
before the attack and at some distance from the
target. Therefore, the accuracy of the weather
forecast will vary with the prediction time and the
distance of the weather observer from the target
area. Meteorological forecasts for specific target
areas will be provided by the AWS upon request.

   6. Artillery Meteorological Units. These units
furnish current weather data. They do not fore-
cast meteorological conditions but make periodic
observations at or near the firing sites.

                                                      layer clothing worn for warmth at low tempera-
                                                      tures serves as a barrier to protect the skin.

                                                      16. Temperature Gradient

                                                         The temperature gradient is an expression of the
                                                      difference in air temperature at two levels. For
                                                      purposes of chemical operations, it is determined
                                                      by subtracting the air temperature measured about
                                                      one-half meter above ground from the air tempera-
                                                      ture about 4 meters above ground. Vertical varia-
                                                      tions in temperature affect air stability, which in
                                                      turn affects the formation of vertical air currents.
                                                      The more stable the air layer in the target area, the
                                                      more effective a chemical attack will be. Under
                                                      unstable conditions, turbulence is great and many
                                                      vertical air currents form, causing chemical clouds
                                                      to disperse quickly. For calculation of chemical
                                                      ammunition requirements, temperature gradient

74. Commander's Risk Criteria                         gree of risk that he will accept for unwarned
                                                      friendly troops downwind of the target area. Risks
   Because of the toxicity of nerve agent vapors      are defined in terms of effects that can influence unit
and aerosols, the commander must decide the de-       effectiveness. The following criteria are used as a
                                                      guide for establishing the degree of risk for ex-
TAQO MM A                                             posure of unmasked friendly troops (table IV) :

                                                         a. Negligible Risk. Troops are reasonably safe
                                                      though there may be some threshold effects. The
                                                      combat effectiveness of units will not be impaired.

                                                         b. Moderate Risk. Anticipated effects are toler-
                                                      able or, at worst, a minor nuisance. There may be
                                                      a few mildly incapacitated casualties and a slight
                                                      reduction in unit combat effectiveness, but units
                                                      will be able to perform assigned missions.

                                                         c. Emergency Risk. Anticipated effects may
                                                      cause some casualties and may significantly reduce
                                                      the unit's combat effectiveness.

                                            •APPENDIX X

               EXAMPLE OF A U.S. MARINE CORPS CHEMICAL FIRE
               SUPPORT PLAN ANNEX TO AN OPERATIONS ORDER

               This example of a U.S. Marine Corps Chemical Fire Support Plan Annex

               to an operations order illustrates a method of planning, coordinating, and

               controlling chemical fires during an amphibious landing on a hostile shore.

               Overlays which would accompany this fire plan are omitted. Abbreviated

               Appendix 1, Chemical Aircraft Spray Munitions Availability, and Appen-

               dix 2, Plan of Chemical Fires, to the fire support plan illustrate the

               formats used.

                                           Copy     of                      copies

                                                 Marine Amphibious Force

                                           and Landing Force (TF33)

                                           c/o FPO

                                           201200Z March 1965

               Annex          (Chemical Fire Suppor Plan) to Opn Plan 4-65

•              Ref: (a) MAP:

                        (b) FMF PAC ORDER  (Chemical Employment SOP)
               Time Zone: G

               *1. SITUATION

               a. Enemy Forces—See Aninex—(Intelligence) to Operation Plan 4-65.

•                 b. Friendly Forces.
                     (1) TF 35 (Attack Carrier Striking Force) on order employs chem-
                           ical munitions against designated targets in accordance with
                           appendix 2 (Plan of Chemical Fires) and appendix 3 (Chemical
                           Employment Overlays).

                     (2) TG 31.3 (Support Carrier Group) on order employs chemical
                           munitions against designated targets in accordance with appen-
                           dix 2 (Plan of Chemical Fires) and appendix 3 (Chemical Em-
                           ployment Overlays).

                     (3) TG 31.4 (Fire Support Group) on order employs chemical muni-
                           tions against designated targets in accordance with appendix 2
                           (Plan of Chemical Fires) and appendix 3 (Chemical Employ-
                           ment Overlays).

               *2. MISSION

                     Chemical delivery agencies employ chemical munitions contingent
                     upon necessity and resultant authorization for employment in support
                     of Landing Force Units (TF 33) in seizing, occupying, and defending
                     positions in the objective area.

   AGO 20034A                                                                               $7


---

Clf FM 3-10/NWIP 36-2/AFM 355-4/FMFM 11-3                                                      •
                                                                                               •
    *3. EXECUTION
                                                                                                 •
       a. Concept of Operations

          (1) Based on necessity and time of receipt of authorization, employ-
                ment of chemical munitions will be as follows :

             (a) Lethal, nonpersistent chemical munitions on D—1 day and
                    D-day.

             (b) Lethal, persistent chemical munitions on D-day.
              (c) Nonlethal, nonpersistent chemical munitions on D-day.
             (d) Individual or collective munitions in above categories as situa-

                    tion dictates subsequent to D-day.
           (2) Employment of chemical munitions is scaled from nonuse on a

                continuum through total employment.

       b. TG 33.1 (3d Mar Div (Reinf)). On order provide chemical fire sup-
          port in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of Chemical Fires).

       c. TF 33.5 (RLT-7). On order establish chemical barriers in accordance
          with appendix 2 (Plan of Chemical Fires) and appendix 3 (Chemical
          Employment Overlays).

       d. TG 33.7 (Landing Force Artillery). On order provide on-call chem-
          ical fires and be prepared to deliver designated chemical fires against
          targets of opportunity in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of Chem-
          ical Fires).

       e. TG 33.6 (U.S. Army Airborne Brigade). On order deliver designated
          chemical fires on targets of opportunity in accordance with appendix
          2 (Plan of Chemical Fires).

       f. TG 33.2 (Landing Force Aviation) (1st MAW). Approximately on
          D—2 days, be prepared to deliver on order chemical fires in accord-
          ance with appendix 2 (Plan of Chemical Fires).

    g. Coordinating Instructions.

    ( 1 ) See reference b.

    (2) Delivery of chemical fires, except chemical agent CS, by landing
          force delivery means on order of Landing Force Commander

          only.

    (3) TG 33.1 coordinate use of agent CS in accordance with refer-
          ence b.

    (4) Troop safety in accordance with appendix 4 (Troop Safety) and
          reference b.

    (5) Reports in accordance with reference b.

    (6) Warning procedures in accordance with reference b and An-

    nex  (Communications-Electronics).

    4. ADMINISTRATION AND LOGISTICS
    See Administrative Plans 4-65.

t  AEROSOL—Consists of a solid or a liquid, not       DOSE—The amount of agent that is taken into
      vaporized, but divided into particles small        or absorbed by the body. Chemical dose is
      enough to float in the air for extended peri-      measured in milligrams.
      ods of time. (A vapor is the gaseous form of
      any substance that is normally a solid or a     HECTARE—10,000 square meters in any con-
      liquid.)                                           figuration.

   CASUALTY THREAT LEVEL—The percent                  MEDIAN DOSAGE
      casualties expected among unprotected
      troops who are expossd to a chemical attack.          a. Median Lethal dosage—The dosage that
                                                               will kill 50 percent of a large group of
   CHEMICAL GB CASUALTY-PRODUCING                              unprotected and exposed troops if they
      DOSAGE—Consists of lethal dosages in the                 do not receive medical treatment in time.
      vicinity of the munition burst and of incapac-
      itating dosages downwind.                             b. Median incapacitating dosage—The dos-
                                                               age that will incapacitate 50 percent of a
   CHEMICAL CASUALTY—A person who has                          large group of unprotected and exposed
      been affected sufficiently by a chemical agent           troops.
      to make him incapable of performing his
      duties or continuing his mission.               PERCUTANEOUS—Means "through the
                                                         skin." Percutaneous effects are achieved by
         a. Immediate chemical casualty—A person         penetration of a person's skin by a chemical
            who becomes a casualty within one hour       agent liquid, vapor, or aerosol.
            after being subjected to a chemical
            attack.                                   PERSISTENCY—Pertains to duration of
                                                         effectiveness; refers to the length of time a
         b. Delayed chemical casualty—A person           chemical agent remains effective in the tar-
            who becomes a casualty more than one         get area after dissemination by a particular
            hour after being subjected to a chemical     agent-munition combination.
            attack.
                                                      SURPRISE DOSAGE ATTACK—A GB chemi-
   CUTANEOUS—Affecting the skin.                         cal fire/strike establish a dosage sufficient to
                                                         produce the desired casualties before troops
   DOSAGE—A measure of the amount of agent               can mask.
      in a given volume of air to which troops are
      exposed for a period of time.                   TOTAL DOSAGE ATTACK—A GB chemical
                                                         fire/strike mission that is not time-limited so
         Chemical dosage—The product of the con-         as to build up the dosage over an extended
      centration of the agent in milligrams per          period of time. This type of attack normally
      cubic meter of air multiplied by the time of       is employed against troops who have no pro-
      exposure in minutss (mg-min/m3).                   tective masks available.

                         APPENDIX X

   f

^                  EXAMPLE OF A U. S. MARINE CORPS B/C FIRE SUPPORT

   '                     PLAN ANNEX TO AN OPERATIONS ORDER

      This example of a U. S. Marine Corps B/C Fire Support Plan Annex to an operations order illustrates

      a method of planning, coordinating, and controlling B/C fires during an amphibious landing on a hostile

      shore. Overlays which would accompany this fire plan are omitted. Abbreviated Appendix 1, B/C Air-

      craft Spray Munitions Availability, and Appendix 2, Plan of B/C Fires, to the fire support plan illustrate

      the formats used.

                                                       Copy     of           copies

                                                             Marine Expeditionary Force and

                                                       Landing Force (TF33)

                                                       c/o FPO

                                                       201200Z March 1965

      Annex— (B/C Fire Support Plan) to Opn Plan 4-65

      Ref: (a) MAP:

      (b) FMF PAC ORDER  (B/C Employment SOP).

      Time Zone: G

      1. Situation

        a. Enemy Forces—See Annex—(Intelligence) to Operation Plan 4-65.

        b. Friendly Forces.

           (1) TF 35 (Attack Carrier Striking Force) on order employs biological and chemical munitions against
                designated targets in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of B/C Fires) and appendix 3 (B/C
                Employment Overlays).

           (2) TG 31.3 (Support Carrier Group) on order employs biological and chemical munitions against
                designated targets in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of B/C Fires) and appendix 3 (B/C
                Employment Overlays).

           (3) TG 31.4 (Fire Support Group) on order employs chemical munitions against designated tar-
                gets in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of B/C Fires) and appendix 3 (B/C Employment
                Overlays).

      2. Mission

        Biological/Chemical delivery agencies employ B/C munitions contingent upon necessity and resultant
      authorization for employment in support of Landing Force Units (TF 33) in seizing, occupying, and
      defending positions in the objective area.

      3. Execution

        a. Concept of Operations.
           (1) Based on necessity and time of receipt of authorization, employment of B/C munitions will be
                as follows:

      TAGO 6654 A                                                                    gj

NWIP 36-2

FMFM 11-3

    (a) Nonlethal, fast-acting biological munitions on D —-3 days.

    (6) Lethal biological munitions on D — 3 days.

    (c) Lethal, nonpersistent chemical munitions on D — 1 day and D-day.

    (d) Lethal, persistent chemical munitions on D-day.

    (e) Nonlethal, nonpersistent chemical munitions on D-day.                                             ' ,

    (/) Nonlethal, slow-acting biological munitions subsequent to D-day.                                     &

    (g) Individual or collective munitions in above categories as situation dictates subsequent to        *

    D-day.

    (2) Employment of B/C munitions is scaled from nonuse on a continuum through total employment.

   b. TG 33.1 (3d Mar Div (Reinf)). On order provide chemical fire support in accordance with appen-
dix 2 (Plan of B/C Fires).

   c. TF 33.5 (RLT-7). On order establish chemical barriers in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of B/C *
Fires) and appendix 3 (B/C Employment Overlays).

   d. TG 33.7 (Landing Force Artillery). On order provide on-call chemical fires and be prepared to de-
liver designated chemical fires against targets of opportunity in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of
B/C Fires).

   e. TG 33.6 (U. S. Army Airborne Brigade). On order deliver designated chemical fires on targets of
opportunity in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of B/C Fires).

   /. TG 33.2 (Landing Force Aviation) (1st MAW). Approximately on D — 2 days, be prepared to de-
liver on order biological and chemical fires in accordance with appendix 2 (Plan of B/C Fires).

g. Coordinating Instructions.
   (1) See reference (b).

    (2) Delivery of B/C fires, except chemical agent CS, by landing force delivery means on order of

    Landing Force Commander only.

    (3) TG 33.1 coordinate use of agent CS in accordance with reference (b).

    (4) Troop safety in accordance with appendix 4 (Troop Safety) and reference (b).

    (5) Reports in accordance with reference (b).

    (6) Warning procedures in accordance with reference (b) and Annex         (Communications-Elec-

    tronics) .

   See Administrative Plans 4-65.
